That suggests render_rows can take a mark_last parameter. But export_csv calls render_rows(rows) without any additional parameters.\n\nThe test_export_appends_totals_row expects export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) to produce a totals row. But export_csv doesn't accept totals parameter. Perhaps the function signature in export.py is outdated, and it should be updated to handle totals.\n\nWe need to fix the export so that when totals=True is passed, it includes a totals row.\n\nLooking at the test assertions:\n\n- test_export_without_totals: export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]]) == \"1,2\\n3,4\"\n- test_export_appends_totals_row: export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) == \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"\n\nSo export_csv should accept an optional `totals` argument (default False). If totals=True, it should append a totals row. The totals row seems to be \"TOTAL,4,6\" where 4 and 6 are the sum of each column? Actually rows are [[1,2],[3,4]]. Column sums: 1+3=4, 2+4=6. So totals row is \"TOTAL,4,6\".\n\nWe need to modify export.py to handle this. Also possibly modify render.py to support marking the last row as total? But the test_render_marks_last_row_as_total suggests render_rows can mark last row as total when mark_last=True. However, the expected output in test_export_appends_totals_row is \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\". That's not just marking the last row as \"TOTAL,3,4\" (which would be from test_render_marks_last_row_as_total). It's a separate totals row with sums.\n\nSo export_csv should:\n\n- If totals=False (default), just render rows as before.\n- If totals=True, render all rows, then compute column totals, and append a row like \"TOTAL,<sum1>,<sum2>,...\"\n\nWe need to import column_totals from totals module perhaps.\n\nLet's examine the imports in test_export.py: it imports column_totals and render_rows. So we can use those.\n\nProposed changes to export.py:\n\n```python\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    if totals:\n        # Render all rows\n        lines = render_rows(rows)\n        # Compute totals\n        tot_values = column_totals(rows)\n        # Create totals row\n        totals_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + [str(v) for v in tot_values]\n        lines.append(\",\".join(totals_row))\n        return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n    else:\n        return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n```\n\nBut wait, render_rows returns a list of strings already formatted as CSV rows.
